./core/Value.pl:# sub Formula  {Value->Package("Formula()")->new(@_)}  # in Parser.pl
./core/scaffold.pl:# Scaffold::Begin() is used to start a new scaffold section, passing
./core/scaffold.pl:# it any options that need to be overridden (e.g. is_open, can_open,
./core/scaffold.pl:# open_first_section, etc).
./core/scaffold.pl:#
./core/scaffold.pl:# Problems can include more than one scaffold, if desired,
./core/scaffold.pl:# and they can be nested.
./core/scaffold.pl:#
./core/scaffold.pl:# We save the current PG_OUTPUT, which will be put back during
./core/scaffold.pl:# the Scaffold::End() call.  The sections use PG_OUTPUT to create
./core/scaffold.pl:# their own text, which is added to the $scaffold->{output}
./core/scaffold.pl:# during the Section::End() call.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Scaffold::End() is used to end the scaffold.
./core/scaffold.pl:#
./core/scaffold.pl:# This puts the scaffold into the page output and opens the sections that should be open.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Then the next nested scaffold (if any) is popped off the stack and returned.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Report an error and die
./core/scaffold.pl:# Create a new Scaffold object.
./core/scaffold.pl:#
./core/scaffold.pl:# Set the defaults for can_open, is_open, etc., but allow
./core/scaffold.pl:# the author to override them.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Add a section to the scaffold and give it a unique number (within
./core/scaffold.pl:# the scaffold).  Determine its label and save it as current_section
./core/scaffold.pl:# so that we know which section is active.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Add the content from the current section into the scaffold's output
./core/scaffold.pl:# and remove the current_section (so we can tell that no section is open).
./core/scaffold.pl:# Record the answers for a section.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Scores are obtained when post processing is done.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Add the given sections to the list of sections to be opened for this scaffold.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Shortcuts for Scaffold data
./core/scaffold.pl:# Section::Begin() is used to start a section in the scaffolding,
./core/scaffold.pl:# passing it the name of the section and any options (e.g., can_open,
./core/scaffold.pl:# is_open, etc.).
./core/scaffold.pl:#
./core/scaffold.pl:# The section is added to the scaffold, and the names of the answer
./core/scaffold.pl:# blanks for previous sections are recorded, along with information
./core/scaffold.pl:# about the answer blanks that have evaluators assigned (so we can
./core/scaffold.pl:# see which answers belong to this section when it closes).
./core/scaffold.pl:# Section::End() is used to end the active section.
./core/scaffold.pl:#
./core/scaffold.pl:# We get the names of the answer blanks that are in this section,
./core/scaffold.pl:# then add the HTML around the section that is used by JavaScript
./core/scaffold.pl:# for showing/hiding the section, and finally tell the scaffold
./core/scaffold.pl:# that the section is complete (it adds the content to its output).
./core/scaffold.pl:# Create a new Section object.
./core/scaffold.pl:#
./core/scaffold.pl:# It takes default values for can_open, is_open, etc.
./core/scaffold.pl:# from the active scaffold.  These can be overridden
./core/scaffold.pl:# by the author.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Adds the necessary HTML around the content of the section.  Initially a temporary "scaffold-section" tag is added that
./core/scaffold.pl:# wraps the content, and that is replaced with the correct HTML in post processing.  The content is also removed in post
./core/scaffold.pl:# processing if the scaffold cannot be opened and is not correct.  The $PG_OUTPUT variable holds just the contents of
./core/scaffold.pl:# this section, so unshift the opening tags onto the front, and push the closing tags onto the back.  (This is added to
./core/scaffold.pl:# the scaffold output when $scaffold->end_section() is called.)
./core/scaffold.pl:# Check if all the answers for this section are correct
./core/scaffold.pl:# Perform the can_open check for this section:
./core/scaffold.pl:#   If the author supplied code, use it, otherwise use the routine from Section::can_open.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Perform the is_open check for this section:
./core/scaffold.pl:#   If the author supplied code, use it, otherwise use the routine from Section::is_open.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Return a boolean array where a 1 means that answer blank has
./core/scaffold.pl:# an answer evaluator assigned to it and 0 means not.
./core/scaffold.pl:# Get the names of any of the original answer blanks that now have evaluators attached.
./core/scaffold.pl:########################################################################
./core/scaffold.pl:# Implements the possible values for the can_open option for scaffolds
./core/scaffold.pl:# and sections
./core/scaffold.pl:# Always can be opened
./core/scaffold.pl:# Can be opened when all the answers from previous sections are correct
./core/scaffold.pl:# Can open when previous are correct but this one is not
./core/scaffold.pl:# Can open when incorrect
./core/scaffold.pl:# Never can be opened
./core/scaffold.pl:########################################################################
./core/scaffold.pl:# Implements the possible values for the is_open option for scaffolds
./core/scaffold.pl:# and sections
./core/scaffold.pl:# Every section is open that can be
./core/scaffold.pl:# Every incorrect section is open that can be
./core/scaffold.pl:# (unless it is the first one, and everything is blank, and
./core/scaffold.pl:# the scaffold doesn't have open_first_section set)
./core/scaffold.pl:# The first incorrect section is open that can be
./core/scaffold.pl:# All correct sections and the first incorrect section
./core/scaffold.pl:# are open (that are allowed to be open)
./core/scaffold.pl:# No sections are open
./core/RserveClient.pl:# This used to take a second $local parameter that specified the name of the file that would be saved in the temp
./core/RserveClient.pl:# directory. That parameter is deprecated and is ignored if given. The problem author should have never been given that
./core/RserveClient.pl:# choice. Furthermore, if the $local parameter was not specified the remote file name was used which changes every time
./core/RserveClient.pl:# the problem is rendered.  That is a problem because it results in a different file being saved into the temporary
./core/RserveClient.pl:# directory every time the problem is rendered.  Instead a unique filename is used that is created via PGresource and
./core/RserveClient.pl:# PGalias (and so is dependent on the problem seed, psvn, problem UUID, etc.).
./core/RserveClient.pl:# Returns an REXP's Perl representation, dereferencing it if it's an array reference.  `REXP::to_perl` returns a string
./core/RserveClient.pl:# scalar for Symbol, undef for Null, and an array reference to contents for all vector types. This function is a utility
./core/RserveClient.pl:# wrapper to make it easy to assign a Vector's representation to an array variable, while still working sensibly for
./core/RserveClient.pl:# non-arrays.

./core/PGanswermacros.pl:## Internal routine that converts variables into the standard array format
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:## IN:	one of the following:
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			an undefined value (i.e., no variable was specified)
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			a reference to an array of variable names -- [var1, var2]
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			a number (the number of variables desired) -- 3
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			one or more variable names -- (var1, var2)
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:## OUT:	an array of variable names

./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:## IN:	one of the following:
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			an undefined value (i.e., no limits were specified)
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			a reference to an array of arrays of limits -- [[llim,ulim], [llim,ulim]]
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			a reference to an array of limits -- [llim, ulim]
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			an array of array references -- ([llim,ulim], [llim,ulim])
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:##			an array of limits -- (llim,ulim)
./core/PGanswermacros.pl:## OUT:	an array of array references -- ([llim,ulim], [llim,ulim]) or ([llim,ulim])

./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# Convert the GraphTool object's options into JSON that can be passed to the JavaScript
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# graphTool method.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# Produce a hidden answer rule to contain the JavaScript result and insert the graphbox div and
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# JavaScript to display the graph tool.  If a hard copy is being generated, then PGtikz.pl is used
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# to generate a printable graph instead.  An attempt is made to make the printable graph look
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# as much as possible like the JavaScript graph.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# Modify the student's list answer returned by the graphTool JavaScript to reproduce the
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# JavaScript graph of the student's answer in the "Answer Preview" box of the results table.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# The raw list form of the answer is displayed in the "Entered" box.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# Create an answer checker to be passed to ANS().  Any parameters are passed to the checker, as
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# well as any parameters passed in via cmpOptions when the GraphTool object is created.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# The correct answer is modified to reproduce the JavaScript graph of the correct answer
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# displayed in the "Correct Answer" box of the results table.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# It is important that the parser::GraphTool object saved in the context flags is not accessed directly in the new
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# method for any package that derives from the GraphTool::GraphObject package.  The objects for correct answers are
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# constructed when the parser::GraphTool "create" method is called, and at that time only the default GraphTool options
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# are available.  If the constructor uses one of those options (for example many of the objects use the bBox option) and
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# that option is later changed when calling the "with" method, then the computations in the constructor will be
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# incorrect and not updated.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# This should return 0 if the $point is satisfies the defining equation of the object or is on an edge of the object.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# Otherwise it should return a nonzero number indicating a side or region of the object that the point is in.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# If $fuzzy is false, then this should return true (or 1) if the $other object is visually the same as this object, and
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# false (or 0) otherwise. If $fuzzy is true, then this should return true if the $other object is visually the same as
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# the object ignoring if one object is solid and the other is dashed, and zero otherwise.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# This makes the == operator work for GraphTool::GraphObjects. It should usually not be overridden.  Instead override
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# the cmp method above.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# The TikZ code to draw the object.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# The TikZ clipping path for the object (used by the inequality fill method) with out the \clip command and its options.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# The TikZ clipping path for the object (used by the inequality fill method) with the \clip command and options. Most
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# objects only override the clipCode method and let this method add in the default \clip command and inverse clip option
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# based on the fillCmp return value.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# This method should return discrete values that represent which region the point ($x, $y) is in of the regions the
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# object breaks the plane into.  The same value must be returned for all points in the same region.  This method should
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# return 0 for all points on a border of the object.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# This is only used by the flood fill algorithm, and should return 1 if $point is on the border of an object, and 0
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# otherwise. This only needs to be overridden if the flood fill algorithm could potentially bleed across a boundary from
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# one region to another (as determined by the return value of the fillCmp method) or the flood fill algorithm could
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# potentially go around an end of the object.
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# This method provides backward compatibility for objects defined the old way not deriving from the
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# GraphTool::GraphObject package. The old methods must be called after construction because they may perform
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# computations using options that are not set to their final values at that time. This is only called once and the
./graph/parserGraphTool.pl:# results cached for later use.

./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:# this accomplishes the following:
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   a) clears the accumulated statistical data,
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   b) Create three random, normally distributed, and one exponentially dist. data sets.
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   c) Add the data set to the collection of data
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   d) initializes a statistical graph object
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   e) adds the relevant box plots.
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:##############################################################################################
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   clear_stat_graph_data();         # (a)

./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   @data1 = urand(10.0,2.0,10,2);   # (b) - mean=10, sd=2.0, N=10, 2 dec places
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   @data2 = urand(12.0,2.0,10,2);   # (b) - mean=12, sd=2.0
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   @data3 = urand(14.0,4.0,10,2);   # (b) - mean=14, sd=4.0
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   @data4 = exprand(0.1,10,2);      # (b) - lambda=0.1, N=10, and 2 dec places (exp)

./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   push_stat_data_set(~~@data1);    # (c)
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   push_stat_data_set(~~@data2);    # (c)
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   push_stat_data_set(~~@data3);    # (c)
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#   push_stat_data_set(~~@data4);    # (c)
./graph/PGstatisticsGraphMacros.pl:#

./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  Create a match list where the answers are images
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  Usage:  $ml = new_image_match_list(options);
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  where options are those that can be supplied to image_print_a below.
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  The answers should be an image name or reference to a plot object
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  (or a reference to a pair of either of these), and they are passed
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  to the Image function for processing.  See unionUtils.pl for more
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  information on how these are handled.
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  A print routine for image matching.  This is designed to display
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  four graphs per row.  More can be included by setting the ImageOptions
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  variable in the match list.  For example:
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#      $ml->{ImageOptions} = [size => [100,100]]
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#  You can include the following options:
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    size => [w,h]      the width and height of the images
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    width => n         the width of the images (obsolete)
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    height => n        the height of the images (obsolete)
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    tex_size => n      the size for the TeX version of the image
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    separation => n    the spacing between the images in a row
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    vseparation => n   the spacing between the images and captions
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    link => 0 or 1     1 to make a link to the original image
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    columns => n       the number of images in each row (defaults to 4)
./graph/imageChoice.pl:#    border => n        the width of the image border

./graph/unionImage.pl:######################################################################
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  A routine to make including images easier to control
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  Usage:  Image(name,options)
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  where name is the name of an image file or a reference to a
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  graphics object (or a reference to a pair of one of these),
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  and options are taken from among the following:
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#    size => [w,h]           the size of the image in the HTML page
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            (default is [150,150])
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#    tex_size => r           the size to use in TeX mode (as a percentage
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            of the line width times 10).  E.g., 500 is
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            half the width, etc.  (default is 200.)
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#    link => 0 or 1          whether to include a link to the original
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            image (default is 0, unless there are
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            two images given)
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#    border => 0 or 1        size of image border in HTML mode
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            (defaults to 2 or 1 depending on whether
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            there is a link or not)
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#    align => placement      vertical alignment for image in HTML mode
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            (default is "BOTTOM")
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#    tex_center => 0 or 1    whether to center the image horizontally
./graph/unionImage.pl:#                            in TeX mode  (default is 0)
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  The image name can be one of a number of different things.  It can be
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  the name of an image file, or an alias to one produce by the alias()
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  command.  It can be a graphics object reference created by init_graph().
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  Or it can be a pair of these (in square brackets).  The first is the
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  image for the HTML file, and the second is the image that it will be
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  linked to.
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  Examples:  Image("graph.gif", size => [200,200]);
./graph/unionImage.pl:#             Image(["graph.gif","graph-large.gif"]);
./graph/unionImage.pl:#
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  The alias() and insertGraph() functions will be called automatically
./graph/unionImage.pl:#  when needed.
./graph/unionImage.pl:#

./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# Observation: a randomly generated 3 x 4 matrix has a very high probability
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# of having rank 3.  So, trying to generate random 3 x 4 matrices until a
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# matrix of rank < 3 appears would be a bad idea.  Also, there is no
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# guarantee that a randomly generated matrix will represent a consistent
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# system; however, empirical evidence suggests that the majority of the
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# time a random 3 x 4 matrix is produced, it is rank 3 and represents
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# a consistent system.  Also, with randomly chosen matrices, it is
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:# possible to get rows or columns of zeros, so watch out!
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:#  rref: input and output are MathObject matrices.
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:#  Should be run in Fraction context for best results.
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:#  This was written by Davide Cervone.
./math/MatrixReduce.pl:#  http://webwork.maa.org/moodle/mod/forum/discuss.php?d=2970

./math/PGnauScheduling.pl:#
./math/PGnauScheduling.pl:# Name : CritPath ( Finds the critical path in a schedule type graph.  'Ties' are broken by comparing
./math/PGnauScheduling.pl:#		    the task labels of the first task at which two paths disagree.  The smaller label
./math/PGnauScheduling.pl:#		    is taken as the critical path. )

./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#!/usr/bin/perl -w
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#my @answer = oldivy(1,2,1,8,4);
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#print ("The old program says:\n");
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#print ($answer[0]);
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#print ("\n");
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#@answer = ivy(1,2,1,8,4);
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#print ("My program says:\n");
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#print ($answer[0]);
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#print ("\n");
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#the subroutine is invoked with arguments such as complexmult(2,3,4,5) or
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#complexmult(@data), where @data = (2,3,4,5)
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:##########
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# sub addtwo adds two strings formally
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# An "indicator"  for a string is a
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#  number ,e.g. coefficient,which indicates
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# whether the string is to be
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# added or is to be regarded as zero.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# The  non-zero terms are formally added as strings.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# The input is an array
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# ($1staddend, $1stindicator,$2ndaddend,$2ndindicator)
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# The return is an array
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# (formal sum, indicator of formal sum)
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:########
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# sub add generalizes sub addtwo to more addends.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# It formally adds the nonzero terms.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# The input is an array of even length
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# consisting of each addend,a string,
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# followed by its indicator.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#######
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# sub diffop cleans up the typed expression
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# of a diff. operator.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# input @diffop =($A,$B,$C) is the coefficients.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# input is given as arguments viz difftop($A,$B,$C);
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# output is the diff. operator as a string $L in TEX
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:########
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# sub rad simplifies (a/b)*(sqrt(c))
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# input is given as arguments on rad viz.: rad($a,$b,$c);
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# $a,$b,$c are integers and $c>=0 and $b is not zero.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# output is an array =(answer as string,new$a,new$b, new$c)
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:##########
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:##########
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:####
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# sub exp simplifies exp($r*t) in form for writing perl
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# or tex. The input is exp($r,$ind); $ind indicates whether
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# we want perl or tex mode. $r is a string that represents
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# a number.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# If $ind = 0  output is "exp(($r)*t)", simplified if possible.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# If $ind = 1  output is "exp(($r)*t)", simplified if possible.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# y(t) = me^(-Bt/(2A))*cos(t*sqrt(4AC-B*B)/(2A))+(2An+Bm)*sqrt(4AC-B*B)/(4AC-B*B)*e^(-Bt/(2A))*sin(t*sqrt(4AC-B*B)/(2A))
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:############
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#sub ivy solves the initial value problem
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# $a*y'' + $b*y' + $c*y = 0, with  y(0) = $m,  y'(0) = $n
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#The numbers $a,$b,$c,$m,$n should be integers with $a not 0.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#The inputs are given as arguments viz: ivy ($a,$b,$c,$m,$n).
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#The output is the solution as a string giving a function of t.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#################
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# undeterminedSin is a subroutine to solve
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# undetermined coefficient problems that have
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# sines and cosines.
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# The input is an array ($A,$B,$C,$r,$w,$q1,$q0,$r1,$r0)
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# given as arguments on undeterminedSin
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# $L =$A y'' + $B y' + $C y
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# $rhs = ($q1 t + $q0) cos($w t)exp($r t) +
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:#        ($r1 t + $r0) sin($w t)exp($r t)
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# The subroutine uses undetermined coefficients
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# to find a solution $y of $L = $rhs .
./math/PGdiffeqmacros.pl:# The output \is $y

./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Implement an "in" operator for "x in (a,b)" as an
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  alternative to inequality notation.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Make sure the variable is to the left and an interval,
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  set, or union is to the right.

./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  right of "if" operators.

./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:##################################################
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  This implements the "in" operator as in inequality.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  We inherit all the inequality methods, and simply
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  need to handle the string and TeX output.  The
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  underlying type is still an Inerval.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:##################################################
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:##################################################
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  This implements the PiecewiseFunction.  It is an unusual mix
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  of a Value object and a Formula object.  It looks like a
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Formula for the most part, but doesn't have the same internal
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  structure.  Most of the Formula methods have been provided
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  so that eval, substitute, reduce, etc will be applied to all
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  the branches.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Create the PiecewiseFunction object, with error reporting
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  for problems in the data.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Usage:  PiecewiseFunction("formula")
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#          PiecewiseFunction(I1 => f1, I2 => f2, ... , fn);
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  In the first case, the formula is parsed for "if" and "else" values
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  to produce the function.  In the second, the function is given
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  by interval/formula pairs that associate what function to map over
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  interval.  If there is an unpaired formula at the end, it is
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  the "otherwise" formula that will be used whenever the input
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  does not fall into one of the given intervals.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Note that the intervals above actually can be Interval, Set,
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  or Union objects, not just plain intervals.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Create a PiecewiseFunction without error checking (so overlapping intervals,
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  incorrect variables, and so on could appear).
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Do the consistency checks for the separate branches.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Check that all the inequalities are for the same variable.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Check that no domain intervals overlap.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Check that all the branches return the same type of result.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  This is always considered a formula.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Look through the branches for the one that contains
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  the variable's value, and evaluate it.  If not in
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  any of the intervals, use the "otherwise" value,
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  or die with no value if there isn't one.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Reduce each branch individually.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Substitute into each branch individually.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  If the function's variable is substituted, then
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#    if it is a constant, find the branch for that value
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#    and substitute into that, otherwise if it is
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#    just another variable, replace the variable
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#    in the inequalities as well as the formulas.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Otherwise, just replace in the formulas.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Return the domain of the function (will be (-inf,inf) if
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  there is an "otherwise" formula.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  The set (-inf,inf).
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  The domain formed by the explicitly given intervals
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  (excludes the "otherwise" portion, if any)
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Creates a copy of the PiecewiseFunction where the "otherwise"
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  formula has been given explicit intervals within the object.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  (This makes it easier to compare two PiecewiseFormulas
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  interval by interval.)
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Look up the function for the nth branch (or the "otherwise"
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  function if n is omitted or too big or too small).
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Look up the domain for the nth branch (or the "otherwise"
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  domain if n is omitted or too big or too small).
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Get the function for the given value of the variable
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  (or undef if there is none).
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Implements the <=> operator (really only handles equality ir not)
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Check that the function domains have the same number of
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  components, and that those components agree, interval by interval.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Now that the intervals are known to agree, compare
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  the individual functions on each interval.  Do an
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  appropriate check depending on the type of each
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  branch:  Interval, Set, or Union.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Use the Interval to determine the limits for use
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  in comparing the two functions.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  For a set, check that the functions agree on every point.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  For a union, do the appropriate check for
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  each object in the union.
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  Stringify using newlines at after each "else".
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  (Otherwise the student and correct answer can
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  get unacceptably long.)
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#
./contexts/contextPiecewiseFunction.pl:#  TeXify using a "cases" LaTeX environment.

./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:######################################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:######################################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  The Currency context has an extra "currency" data
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  type (like flags, variables, etc.)
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  It also creates some patterns needed for handling
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  currency values, and sets the Parser and Value
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  hashes to activate the Currency objects.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  The tolerance is set to .005 absolute so that
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  answers must be correct to the penny.  You can
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  change this in the context, or for individual
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  currency values.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:##################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  This is the context data for currency.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  A special pattern is maintained for the
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  comma form of numbers (using the specified
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  comma and decimal-place characters).
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  You specify the currency symbol via
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#    Context()->currency->set(symbol=>'$');
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#    Context()->currency->set(comma=>',',decimal=>'.');
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  You can add extra symbols via
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#    Context()->currency->addSymbol("dollar","dollars");
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  If the symbol contains alphabetic characters, it
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  is made to be right-associative (i.e., comes after
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  the number), otherwise it is left-associative (i.e.,
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  before the number).  You can change that for a
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  symbol using
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#    Context()->currency->setSymbol("Euro"=>{associativity=>"left"});
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Finally, an extra symbol can be removed with
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#    Context()->currency-removeSymbol("dollar");
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Set up the initial data
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Create, set and remove extra currency symbols
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Update the currency patterns in case the characters have changed,
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  and if the symbol has changed, remove the old operator(s) and
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  create a new one for the given symbol.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:######################################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:######################################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  When creating Number objects in the Parser, we need to remove the
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  comma (and currency) characters and replace the decimal character
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  with an actual decimal point.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:##################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  This class implements the currency symbol.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  It checks that its operand is a numeric constant
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  in the correct format, and produces
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  a Currency object when evaluated.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Use the Currency MathObject to produce the output formats
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:######################################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:######################################################################
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  This is the MathObject class for currency objects.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  It is basically a Real(), but one that stringifies
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  and texifies itself to include the currency symbol
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  and commas every three digits.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  We need to override the new() and make() methods
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  so that the Currency object will be counted as
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  a Value object.  If we aren't promoting Reals,
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  produce an error message.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Look up the currency symbols either from the object of the context
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  and format the output as a currency value (use 2 decimals and
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  insert commas every three digits).  Put the currency symbol
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  on the correct end for the associativity and remove leading
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  and trailing spaces.
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Override the class name to get better error messages
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#  Add promoteReals option to allow Reals with no dollars
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:#
./contexts/contextCurrency.pl:######################################################################

./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  Sets all the default contexts to use alternate decimals.
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  The two arguments determine the values for the
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  enterDecimals and displayDecimals flags.  If enterDecimals
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  is ",", then student answers must use commas for decimals
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  (though professors can use either).  If the display is not
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  ".", then separators for lists, points, vectors, etc, are
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  displayed as ";".
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:###########################################################
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  Handle numbers with commas as decimal indicators, and produce an
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  error if the format is not what is allowed.  Mark the number if it
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  is in the alternate form, so we can display it correctly later.  If
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  needed, save the original string, swap the comma for a dot, save
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  THAT, and correct the number's numeric value.
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  If we have an alternate form, make a Real out of it so that
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  we can retain the alternateForm flag.  (I'm not sure if there
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  will be any problems from that, since it usually only returns
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#  a Perl real).
./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#

./contexts/contextAlternateDecimal.pl:#

./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  This package provides create() and extend() functions that can be
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  used to get a copy of an existing context and extend it by
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  overriding the existing classes with your own, while maintaining
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  information about those original classes so that you can fall back
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  on them for any situations that don't involve your new
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  functionality.  These functions are designed so that multiple
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  extensions can be added without interfering with one another.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  ID to use for contexts that need a dynamic extension
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Copy the given context (given by name or as a Context object)
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  and name the new one.  For example,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    $context = context::Extensions::create("Quaternions", "Complex");
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  would create a context named "Quaternions-Complex" as a copy of the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Complex context.  The implementation for classes added to this
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  context should be in the context::Quaternions namespace.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Extend a given Context object to include new features by specifying
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  classes to use for operators, functions, value object, and parser
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  objects, while retaining the old classes for fallback use.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  The changes are specified in the options following the Context, and these
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  can include:
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    opClasses => { op => 'class', ... }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies the operators to override, and the class suffix to
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      use for their implementations.  For example, using
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#         opClasses => { '+' => 'BOP::add' }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      would attach the class context::Quaternions::BOP::add to the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      plus sign in our Quaternion setting.  If the space operator (' ')
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      is in your list, and if the original context has it point to an
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      operator that is NOT in your list, then that referenced operator
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      is redirected automatically to 'BOP::Space' in your base context
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      package.  In our case, we would want to include a definition for
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      context::Quaternions::BOP::Space in order to cover that possibility.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    ops => { op => {def}, ... }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies new operators to add to the context (where "def" is
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      an operator definition like those for any context).
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    functions => 'class1|class2|...'
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies the function categories that you are overriding (e.g.,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#         functions => 'numeric|trig|hyperbolic'
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      would override the functions that have classes that end in
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      ::Functions:numeric, ::Function::trig, or ::Function::hyperbolic
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      and direct them to your own versions of these.  In our quaternion
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      setting that would be to context::Quaternions::Function::numeric
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      for the first of these, and similarly for the others.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    value => ['Class1', 'Class2', ...]
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies the Value object classes to override.  For instance,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#         value => ['Real', 'Formula']
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      would set $context->{value}{Real} and $context->{value}{Formula}
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      to point to your own versions of these (e.g., in our example,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      these would be context::Quaternions::Value::Real and
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      context::Quaternions::Value::Formula.  Note that if you list
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      the parenthesized version (used by the corresponding constructor
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      functions), then the parentheses are replaced by "_Parens" in the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      class name.  For example,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#         value => ['Real()']
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      would set
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#         $context->{value}{Real()} = 'context::Quaternions::Value::Real_Parens';
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    parser => ['Class1', 'Class2', ... ]
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies the Parser classes to override.  This works similarly
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      to the "value" option above, so that
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#         parser => ['Number']
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      would set $context->{parser}{Number} to your version of this class,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      which would be context::Quaternions::Parser::Number in our example.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    flags => { flag => value, ...}
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies the new flags to add to the context (or existing ones to
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      override.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    reductions => { name => 1 or 0, ... }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies new reduction rules to add to the context, and
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      whether they are in effect by default (1) or not (0).  Of
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      course, you need to implement these reduction rules in your
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      Parser objects.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    context => "Context"
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      specifies that your context is a subclass of Parser::Context
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      that adds methods to the context.  If specified, the modified
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      context will be blessed using this value as the suffix for the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      context's class.  In our quaternion example, the value "Context"
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      would mean the resulting modified context would be blessed
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      as context::Quaternions::Context.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  The extend() function returns the modified context.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  The various operators, functions, and value and Parser objects that
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  you define should use the context::Extensions::Super package below
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  in order to access the original classes for those objects.  Ideally,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  your new objects will mutate (i.e., re-bless) themselves to their
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  original classes if they don't involve your new MathObjects.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  For example, the new context::Quaternions::BOP::add class should
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  have the context::Extensions::Super object as one of its
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  superclasses, and then its _check() method could check if either
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  operand is a quaternion, and if not, it can call
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  $self->mutate->_check to turn itself into the original object's
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  class and perform its _check() actions.  That way, the new BOP::add
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  class only needs to worry about implementing the situation for
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  quaternions, and lets the original class deal with everything else.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Record original operator class and set the new one,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  extending to a new class if needed.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Record original class for a given Value or Parser class
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  A common class for getting the super-class of an extension class.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  This class handles all the details of dealing with the original
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  object classes that you have overridden in the context.  You should
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  create a subclass of this class and define its extensionContext()
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  method to return your base context name, and then include that
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  subclass in your @ISA arrays for your new classes that override the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  original context's classes.  (This is not strictly necessary, but
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  it is more efficient to do this than to have the Super class
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  have to figure it out every time a Super method is used.)
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  For our quaternions example, you would use
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    package context::Quaternions::Super
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    our @ISA = ('context::Extensions::Super');
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    sub extensionContext { 'context::Quaternions' }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  and then use 'context::Quaternions::Super' in the @ISA of your new
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  classes for operators, functions, or Value or Parser objects.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  E.g.,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    package context::Quaternions::BOP::add;
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    our @ISA = ('context::Quaternions::Super', 'Parser::BOP');
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    sub _check {
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      my $self = shift;
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      return $self->mutate->_check
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#        unless $self->{lop}->class eq 'Quaternion' || $self->{rop}->class eq 'Quaternion';
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      #  Do your checking for proper arguments to go along with a quaternion here
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    sub _eval {
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      #  Do what is needed to perform addition between quaternions or between
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      #  a quaternion or another legal value here.  You don't have to worry
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      #  about any other types here, as the mutate() call above will change
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      #  the class to the original class (and its _eval() method) if one
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      #  of the operands isn't a quaternion.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  If you need to call a method from the original class, use
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    &{$self->super("method")}($self, args...);
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  where "method" is the name of the method to call, and "args" are any arguments
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  that you need to pass.  For example,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    my $string = &{$self->super("string")}($self);
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  would get the string output from the original class.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  If you are defining a new() or make() method (where the $self could be
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  the class name rather than a class instance), you will need to pass the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  context to mutate(), super(), or superClass().  See the example for
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  new() below.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  The superClass() method gets you the name of the original class, in
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  case you need to access any class variables from that.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Get a method from the original class from the extended context
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Get the super class name from the extension hash in the context
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Re-bless the current object to become the other object,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  if there is one, or the object's super class if not.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Use the super-class new() method
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Get the object's class from its class name
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  This method assumes the extension is in a class named
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  "context::<name>" where <name> is replaced by the name of the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  context.  E.g., context::Quaternions in our example.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  That assumption can be changed by subclassing
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  context::Extensions::Super package and overriding this method with
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  one that returns the extension context's name.  It is more efficient
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  to do that, anyway, but you can get away without it.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  A common class for handling the private extension data in an object's typeRef.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  This allows you to add and retrieve custom data to and from an
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  object's type in such a way that it doesn't interfere with the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  original object's type, or that of any other extensions.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  A MathObject's typeRef property is a HASH that includes information
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  about the object's type, its length (for things like lists and
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  vectors), and entry types (again for objects like lists and
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  vectors).  We can add data to this hash to store additional
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  information that we need in order to be more granular about the
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  type or class of a Parser object.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  To use this, create a subclass of context::Extensions::Data that
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  has an extensionID() method that returns a name to use as the hash
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  key to store your custom data (the default is to use the base
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  context name).  Your subclass should also include your Super class
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  as a parent class.  For example:
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    package context::Quaternions::Data;
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    our @ISA = ('context::Quaternions::Super', 'context::Extensions:Data');
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    sub extensionID { 'quatData' }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Then use this new subclass in the @ISA list for any class that needs access
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  to your custom data.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  The extensionData() method returns the complete hash of your custom
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  data, from which you can extract the value of the property you
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  need, or can set any properties that you want.  E.g.,
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    $self->extensionData->{class};
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  could be used to obtain the custom "class" property of your data.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  The setExtensionType() method is used to set an object's
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  $self->{type} property (which holds the object's typeRef) to a
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  named type residing in your base context.  For example:
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    package context::Quaternions;
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    our $QUATERNION = Value::Type("Number, undef, undef, quatData => {class => "QUATERNION"});
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    package context::Quaternions::Super
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    our @ISA = ('context::Extensions::Super');
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    sub extensionContext { 'context::Quaternions' }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    package context::Quaternions::Data;
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    our @ISA = ('context::Quaternions::Super', 'context::Extensions:Data');
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    sub extensionID { 'quatData' }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    package context::Quaternions::BOP::add;
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    our @ISA = ('context::Quaternions::Data', 'Parser::BOP');
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    sub _check {
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      my $self = shift;
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      unless $self->{lop}->class eq 'Quaternion' || $self->{rop}->class eq 'Quaternion';
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      #  other type checking here
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#      $self->setExtensionType("QUATERNION");  # Use the type in the $QUATERNION variable above
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    }
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Finally, the extensionDataMatch() method checks if the value of a
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  given property is one of a set of values.  For example, if you have
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  a property called "class", then
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#    $self->extensionDataMatch($self->{lop}, "class", "QUATERNION", "COMPLEX");
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  would return 1 if the quatData->{class} was either "QUATERNION" or
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  "COMPLEX" in the $self->{lop}{type} hash, and 0 otherwise.
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Get the object's extensionData
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Set the object's extensionData (and the rest of its type)
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  Check if an object's extension property matches one of the given values
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#  The extension context can subclass that is produce a better name
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################
./contexts/contextExtensions.pl:#################################################################################################

./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#

./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Here we set up the prototype contexts and define the needed
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  functions in the main:: namespace.  Some error messages are
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  modified to read better for these contexts.
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  A routine to set the letters allowed in this context.
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  (Old letters are cleared, and > and = are allowed, but hidden,
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#   since they are used in the List() objects that implement the context).
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Create orderings from strings or lists of letter => value pairs.
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  A copy of the current context is created that contains the proper
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  letters, and the correct string is created and parsed into an
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Ordering object.
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#############################################################
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  This is a Parser BOP used to create the Ordering objects
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  used internally.  They are actually lists with the operator
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  and the two operands, and the comparisons is based on the
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  standard list comparisons.  The operands are either the strings
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  for individual letters, or another Ordering object as a
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  nested List.
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#############################################################
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  This is the Value object used to implement the list That represents
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  one ordering operation.  It is simply a normal Value::List with the
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  operator as the first entry and the two operands as the remaing
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  entries in the list.  The new() method is overriden to make binary
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  trees of equal operators into flat sorted lists.  We override the
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  List string and TeX methods so that they print correctly as binary
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  operators.  The cmp_equal method is overriden to make sure the that
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  the lists are treated as a unit during answer checking.  There is
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  also a routine for adding letters to the object's context.
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Put all equal letters into one list and sort them
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Make sure we do comparison as a list of lists (rather than as the
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  individual entries in the underlying Value::List that encodes
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  the ordering)
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Add more letters to the ordering's context (so student answers
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  can include them even if they aren't in the correct answer).
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#############################################################
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  This overrides the TeX method of the letters
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  so that they don't print using the \rm font.
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#############################################################
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Override Parser classes so that we can check for repeated letters
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Save the letters positional reference
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#########################
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Move letters to Value object
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Return Ordering class if the object is one
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#############################################################
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  This overrides the cmp_equal method to make sure that
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  Ordering lists are put into nested lists (since the
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  underlying ordering is a list, we don't want the
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  list checker to test the individual parts of the list,
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#  but rather the list as a whole).
./contexts/contextOrdering.pl:#

./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#
./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#  These give the fundamental units of the unit names to be added to
./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#  the context, or a list of such, or a list of names of known units.
./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#  If a name begins with a dash, then REMOVE the category or named
./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#  unit.  For example, the "length" category excludes the lengths that
./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#  are part of the "atomics" and "astronomy" categories.  If a name

./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#  allowed, and set the type if they are.
./contexts/contextUnits.pl:#

./parsers/parserImplicitEquation.pl:#  the professor's function on the student's test points and the
